Drop-seq → CITE-seq · method_development

Retained / shared arrangement

Scoped development of the Macosko Drop-seq assay into the Drop-seq implementation of Stoeckius CITE-seq. Retained arrangement: co-encapsulate a cell and a distinctly barcoded oligo(dT) bead, lyse and capture cellular poly(A) mRNA in the droplet, break the emulsion and copy/amplify captured templates with bead-derived cell identity and UMIs. The RNA branch retains Drop-seq tagmentation for eventual expression-library preparation. The affected portion is cell preparation through cell-barcoded DNA preparation and product routing, not every component of CITE-seq or all CITE-seq platforms. The selected whole groups have different terminal materials; they are not asserted to be equivalent transformations.

What changed

Retained arrangement is specified above. Distinct changes and boundary qualifications follow. 1. Entry and recording reagent (left load; right bind_antibodies -> encapsulate): the left assay loads a cell suspension to record cellular mRNAs; it has no DNA-tagged surface-antibody input in this assay. The right first stains cells with prepared antibody–DNA complexes and washes away unbound antibodies. The reporter carries an antibody-identity sequence, a specific amplification handle and a synthetic 3′ poly(A) tail. Its 5′ attachment uses streptavidin–biotin with a disulfide link permissive for release in reducing conditions. Thus the next encapsulation operation receives cell-associated reporters as well as intracellular RNA. Reporter manufacture and a timed cleavage step are not invented operations. Support: left Results b00009–b00011; right b00014, b00025, b00034. 2. Capture substrate and measurement (left lyse_capture; right lyse_capture): left poly(A) cellular RNA hybridizes to bead oligo(dT); right cellular RNA AND synthetic poly(A) DNA reporters hybridize to the same type of bead. The comparable poly(A):poly(T) capture interaction does not turn the two input classes into one substrate: RNA makes RNA–DNA hybrids, reporter DNA makes DNA–DNA hybrids. The added DNA sequence identifies an antibody bound to a surface epitope; the protein is not copied or sequenced. Non-poly(A) RNA drawn in the right droplet is lysate context, not an additional captured branch. Support: left b00011 and Figure 2A b00112; right b00014, b00029. 3. Copying and attribution (left recover_pool -> make_library; right make_barcoded_dna): left recovery precedes bulk RNA-dependent reverse transcription, yielding mRNA-derived cDNA carrying the bead-primer cell barcode and UMI. Right retains this RNA branch and uses the reverse transcriptase's DNA-dependent DNA polymerase activity to copy the antibody DNA in the same preparation; both product classes acquire the bead's cell identity. The antibody-identity barcode is distinct from the bead cell barcode and molecular identifier. Early human/mouse reporter designs also contained UMIs that the authors call redundant for Drop-seq/10× because those preparations already supply UMIs. This is not evidence that RNA and DNA templates use identical template-switch paths or have identical conversion rates. Support: left b00011, b00017 and b00112; right b00014, b00029. 4. Product branching and library interface (left make_library; right make_barcoded_dna -> size_separate): left makes an mRNA-derived 3′ expression library using Nextera XT and custom primers, without an antibody-tag fraction. Right initially amplifies the mixed products for 10 cycles, THEN separates <300 nt ADTs from >300 nt mRNA-derived cDNA. The order matters: size_separate receives an initially amplified mixture, not intact RNA or protein. The short products retain antibody identity and cell tags; the long products retain transcript-derived sequence and cell tags. The paper's subsequent ADT-specific amplification appends P5/P7; the mRNA fraction retains tagmentation as described in Drop-seq. These subsequent steps explain the two fraction interfaces but lie beyond the right diagram. Separate libraries permit independent proportions when pooled for sequencing; the reported Drop-seq–CITE-seq run used 10% ADT/90% cDNA. The paper also mentions direct ADT amplification from washed beads as an alternative; that alternative is not this drawn size-separated route. Support: left b00056; right b00014 and b00036. 5. Loading context (left load; right encapsulate): original Drop-seq species-mixing validation examined 12.5–100 cells/µL and related concentration to doublets and purity. The CITE-seq Drop-seq human/mouse test instead used 400 cells/µL deliberately to obtain many multiplets, with 150 cells/µL reported for PBMC experiments. This is a documented experimental context change, not a general throughput or purity improvement. Support: left b00022–b00024; right b00015, b00036. 6. Drawing endpoints, not absence of a process: the left selected preparation ends in a sequencing library and is followed by the existing sequence operation. The right selected group ends in TWO DNA material fractions; its caption about coupled expression and surface-marker information is not a sequencing operation or data state. The papers report NextSeq 500 on the left and HiSeq 2500 Rapid Run v2 for the right Drop-seq implementation, but the right has no operation ID to anchor a sequencing correspondence. Those instrument descriptions are downstream context, not an additional directed transfer claim. Support: left b00056 and Figure 2C–D b00112; right b00014, b00036.

Reported consequence and limits

Demonstrated consequence of the combined antibody-labeling/capture/copying workflow, not an isolated gain assigned to a reagent: in the Drop-seq CITE-seq HeLa/4T1 CD29 species-mixing experiment (deliberately high loading, 400 cells/µL), 97.2% of transcriptome-defined human, mouse or mixed droplets received the same species classification by ADT counts (right b00015, b00036). This validates coupled cell attribution in that test; it is not a sensitivity, doublet-rate or RNA-yield comparison against original Drop-seq. For relative quantitative protein interpretation, the right paper compared CD8a FACS-defined cord-blood pools, split for flow cytometry and CITE-seq using Drop-seq (b00017, b00044), and reported similar relative expression levels. Independent library proportions are the authors' stated means to allocate depth (b00014), not a demonstrated improvement in molecule recovery. Neither paper establishes equal RNA performance or a causal gain for each coordinated change. The 8,005-cell CBMC multimodal/NK-subset results belong to the right paper's 10× implementation (b00018–b00020, b00038), not a measured benefit of this particular Drop-seq route.

Reviewer reasoning

Accept the submitted method_development claim at its expressly limited Drop-seq implementation scope. The shared definition met is a documented starting assay with a retained and revised connected arrangement, rather than adoption of an isolated contribution. In the later source, b00036 attributes the performed procedure and retained mRNA-library tagmentation to reference 1; b00014 and Figure 1b (b00099) identify the Drop-seq apparatus, microparticles and actual combination. Reference 1 is independently identified in the registered bibliography and b00071 as Macosko et al., PMID 26000488, DOI 10.1016/j.cell.2015.05.002. The introductory reference-1 citation alone would not establish this relation, but the procedural attribution and actual implementation do. Direction follows that attribution, not the publication dates. Scoped reconstruction: Macosko b00009, b00011 and b00015 establish cell suspension plus bead-attached primers carrying a constant region, bead-specific cell barcode, primer-specific UMI and terminal oligo(dT), co-compartmentalized with lysis buffer. Lysis releases cellular mRNA, whose poly(A) tails hybridize to the bead primers in droplets. Earlier b00017, b00056 and Figure 2A (b00112) then establish emulsion disruption, collection/washing of the mRNA-bearing beads, bulk RNA copying from the attached primers, a downstream template-switch PCR handle, amplification and Nextera XT preparation of 3′ expression libraries. The resulting DNA library serves the left sequence operation, whose paired reads recover cell/UMI and transcript information (Figure 2C). This earlier arrangement was checked in the earlier paper, not reconstructed solely from CITE-seq. The later b00014, b00025 and b00034 introduce the coordinated changes described in the claim: antibody-associated DNA with an antibody-identity barcode, amplification handle and synthetic 3′ poly(A) tail; staining and removal of unbound antibodies before the labeled cells reach the retained droplet/bead arrangement. Streptavidin–biotin and a disulfide-bearing attachment describe the incoming reagent; the claim neither invents manufacture operations nor requires an unestablished cleavage order. The same later b00014 explicitly connects in-droplet co-capture of mRNA and reporter DNA to copying with the companion bead's cell identity. Later b00029 supplies the necessary material distinction: cellular RNA templates cDNA, whereas synthetic DNA reporters are copied by DNA-dependent polymerase activity of reverse transcriptase. Bead-derived cell identity and UMIs do not replace the separate antibody-identity barcode. These are separate molecular products sharing cell identity, not a fused RNA/protein product or direct protein sequence measurement. Later b00036 connects the mixed products to ten initial amplification cycles and then size fractionation into shorter ADTs and longer cellular-mRNA-derived cDNA. Both output branches are necessary and are included. The later ADT-specific P5/P7 amplification and retained mRNA tagmentation explain what the fractions subsequently serve, but are correctly treated as downstream paper annotations outside the right graph. Independent library mixing proportions are an author-stated depth-allocation facility, not demonstrated increased recovery. The alternative direct-from-washed-bead ADT amplification is explicitly distinguished from the depicted size-separated route. The higher right loading is correctly recorded as an intentional multiplet-rich validation context, rather than a purity improvement. All exact selected global Operation IDs were checked with the supplied group tool. The left four-operation group has complete inputs cells and beads, internal droplets/captured_droplets/pooled_beads, output library and an outgoing boundary to sequence. The right five-operation group has inputs cells, antibody_dna and oligobeads and ends in both mrna_dna_fraction and adt_dna_fraction, with no hidden outgoing operation. Different whole-group endpoints therefore do not invalidate this expressly qualified historical reconstruction. No additional component_adoption label duplicates the assertion. The consequence is appropriately limited. Later b00015's 97.2% is species-classification concordance between transcript and ADT assignments, including deliberately generated multiplets in the HeLa/4T1 CD29 experiment. It supports the combined implementation in that experiment without proving pure one-cell origin for every barcode, absolute protein abundance, an isolated chemical benefit or an RNA-efficiency gain over Macosko. The CD8a relative-readout comparison uses the Drop-seq route (b00017, b00044); the 8,005-cell CBMC/NK results use 10× (b00018–b00020, b00038). The submitted claim preserves these different comparators and implementations. The necessary changes at this claim's scope are covered; no missing change requiring revision was identified.

        "source_id": "left:drop-seq::paper:26000488",
        "quote": "Drop-Seq consists of the following steps (Figure 1A): (1) prepare a single-cell suspension from a tissue; (2) co-encapsulate each cell with a distinctly barcoded microparticle (bead) in a nanoliter-scale droplet; (3) lyse cells after they have been isolated in droplets; (4) capture a cell’s mRNAs on its companion microparticle, forming STAMPs (Single-cell Transcriptomes Attached to Microparticles); (5) reverse-transcribe, amplify, and sequence thousands of STAMPs in one reaction; and (6) use the STAMP barcodes to infer each transcript’s cell of origin."
      },
      {
        "source_id": "left:drop-seq::paper:26000488",
        "quote": "Each oligonucleotide is composed of four parts (Figure 1B): (1) a constant sequence (identical on all primers and beads) for use as a priming site for downstream PCR and sequencing; (2) a “cell barcode” (identical across all the primers on the surface of any one bead, but different from the cell barcodes on other beads); (3) a Unique Molecular Identifier (UMI) (different on each primer, to identify PCR duplicates) (Kivioja et al., 2012); and (4) an oligo-dT sequence for capturing polyadenylated mRNAs and priming reverse transcription."

Earlier b00017, b00056 and Figure 2A (b00112) then establish emulsion disruption, collection/washing of the mRNA-bearing beads, bulk RNA copying from the attached primers, a downstream template-switch PCR handle, amplification and Nextera XT preparation of 3′ expression libraries. The resulting DNA library serves the left sequence operation, whose paired reads recover cell/UMI and transcript information (Figure 2C). This earlier arrangement was checked in the earlier paper, not reconstructed solely from CITE-seq.\n\nThe later b00014, b00025 and b00034 introduce the coordinated changes described in the claim: antibody-associated DNA with an antibody-identity barcode, amplification handle and synthetic 3′ poly(A) tail; staining and removal of unbound antibodies before the labeled cells reach the retained droplet/bead arrangement. Streptavidin–biotin and a disulfide-bearing attachment describe the incoming reagent; the claim neither invents manufacture operations nor requires an unestablished cleavage order. The same later b00014 explicitly connects in-droplet co-capture of mRNA and reporter DNA to copying with the companion bead's cell identity. Later b00029 supplies the necessary material distinction: cellular RNA templates cDNA, whereas synthetic DNA reporters are copied by DNA-dependent polymerase activity of reverse transcriptase. Bead-derived cell identity and UMIs do not replace the separate antibody-identity barcode. These are separate molecular products sharing cell identity, not a fused RNA/protein product or direct protein sequence measurement.\n\nLater b00036 connects the mixed products to ten initial amplification cycles and then size fractionation into shorter ADTs and longer cellular-mRNA-derived cDNA. Both output branches are necessary and are included. The later ADT-specific P5/P7 amplification and retained mRNA tagmentation explain what the fractions subsequently serve, but are correctly treated as downstream paper annotations outside the right graph. Independent library mixing proportions are an author-stated depth-allocation facility, not demonstrated increased recovery. The alternative direct-from-washed-bead ADT amplification is explicitly distinguished from the depicted size-separated route. The higher right loading is correctly recorded as an intentional multiplet-rich validation context, rather than a purity improvement.\n\nAll exact selected global Operation IDs were checked with the supplied group tool. The left four-operation group has complete inputs cells and beads, internal droplets/captured_droplets/pooled_beads, output library and an outgoing boundary to sequence. The right five-operation group has inputs cells, antibody_dna and oligobeads and ends in both mrna_dna_fraction and adt_dna_fraction, with no hidden outgoing operation. Different whole-group endpoints therefore do not invalidate this expressly qualified historical reconstruction. No additional component_adoption label duplicates the assertion.\n\nThe consequence is appropriately limited. Later b00015's 97.2% is species-classification concordance between transcript and ADT assignments, including deliberately generated multiplets in the HeLa/4T1 CD29 experiment. It supports the combined implementation in that experiment without proving pure one-cell origin for every barcode, absolute protein abundance, an isolated chemical benefit or an RNA-efficiency gain over Macosko. The CD8a relative-readout comparison uses the Drop-seq route (b00017, b00044); the 8,005-cell CBMC/NK results use 10× (b00018–b00020, b00038). The submitted claim preserves these different comparators and implementations. The necessary changes at this claim's scope are covered; no missing change requiring revision was identified.",

        "quote": "Drop-Seq consists of the following steps (Figure 1A): (1) prepare a single-cell suspension from a tissue; (2) co-encapsulate each cell with a distinctly barcoded microparticle (bead) in a nanoliter-scale droplet; (3) lyse cells after they have been isolated in droplets; (4) capture a cell’s mRNAs on its companion microparticle, forming STAMPs (Single-cell Transcriptomes Attached to Microparticles); (5) reverse-transcribe, amplify, and sequence thousands of STAMPs in one reaction; and (6) use the STAMP barcodes to infer each transcript’s cell of origin."

CITE-seq → ECCITE-seq · method_development

Retained / shared arrangement

Retained arrangement: DNA-oligo-labeled antibodies bind surface epitopes before droplet single-cell RNA processing; transcript-derived DNA and antibody-identity DNA acquire a common cell barcode, are amplified together initially, and are physically separated into long cellular cDNA and short reporter fractions for independent downstream readout. The historical assertion concerns revision of this CITE-seq protein/transcript co-assay strategy, from labeling through the size split, rather than inheritance of the Drop-seq apparatus, capture chemistry, or every later ECCITE-seq branch.

What changed

1. Antibody reporter input (left bind_antibodies; right stain_A, stain_B, stain_C): Stoeckius CITE-seq links the oligo's 5′ end through streptavidin–biotin with a reducible disulfide and uses a 3′ poly(A) capture tail. Mimitou ECCITE-seq uses covalent, irreversible iEDDA-click antibody–DNA conjugates and a tag sequence partially complementary to the 5P bead TSO. These are changed input reporter attachment and capture features, not additional manufacture or cleavage operations. The left paper says release may be unnecessary in some applications; the right does not establish a release reaction. References 21 and 24, not reference 7 alone, are the right paper's conjugation attributions (left b00014/b00025; right b00011/b00024). 2. Capture/barcode interface (left encapsulate, lyse_capture, make_barcoded_dna; right encapsulate, lyse, rt_barcode): left mRNA and antibody DNA both anneal through their 3′ poly(A) tails to bead-bound poly(T), whose primer carries the cell identifier. Right mRNA RT is primed by soluble poly(dT), RNA-derived cDNA receives cell barcode and UMI by template switching with bead-derived TSO, and antibody DNA receives these identifiers by direct annealing/extension at the TSO interface in the same droplet. The 5P workflow is therefore a coordinated change in primer placement and reporter acceptance, not addition of the same bead capture reaction. Both RNA and synthetic DNA reporters ultimately reach DNA libraries; proteins themselves do not (left b00014/b00029; right b00011/b00097). 3. Sample attribution (left bind_antibodies versus right stain_A/B/C and pool): left antibody barcodes identify the targeted antibody; no sample-hashtag route is depicted or demonstrated in this earlier co-assay. Right separately labels the input sample populations with hashtag antibodies as well as protein-marker antibodies before combining cells; different amplification handles distinguish hashtag from protein-tag DNA. A hashtag sequence reports original sample, an antibody barcode reports marker identity, and the subsequently supplied cell barcode joins modalities. These identifiers have different meanings. A/B/C are diagram sample instances, not evidence of particular species, guides, or a fixed number of samples in every application (left b00014; right b00008/b00011/b00012). 4. Direct expressed-guide recording (left lyse_capture/make_barcoded_dna; right encapsulate/rt_barcode): the left scope captures polyadenylated mRNA and antibody DNA, with no demonstrated sgRNA reporter. Right additionally supplies a primer complementary to the invariant 3′ S. pyogenes Cas9 sgRNA scaffold when guide capture is desired; copying the 5′ variable guide and template switching yields cell-barcoded, UMI-bearing guide cDNA. This records the actual expressed sgRNA sequence, not a linked polyadenylated proxy and not proof of the genomic edit. The new guide-tag product must survive recover_amplify and enter the short fraction for prepare_guide; generic non-poly(A) RNA in the poster is not evidence for capture of all such RNAs (left b00014; right b00011/b00029–32). No sgRNA ancestry is assigned to CITE-seq. 5. Product generation and partition (left make_barcoded_dna/size_separate; right rt_barcode/recover_amplify/size_separate): left initial amplification is included in the broad DNA-generation block, followed in the Drop-seq methods by Ampure separation of <300 nt ADTs from >300 nt mRNA cDNA. Right explicitly barcodes within droplets before the reviewed recovery/amplification block, adds protein-, hashtag- and optionally guide-tag primers during cDNA amplification, and uses 0.6X SPRI to retain large cDNA on beads while all three short reporter classes remain together in supernatant. Right size selection does not produce three pure tag fractions. The primer additions, mixed short products and later handle-specific PCR work together; there is no isolated-effect experiment assigning a gain to one of them (left b00036; right b00011/b00030–32). 6. Downstream meaning at the inspected group boundary: left size_separate ends the graph at mRNA-derived and ADT DNA fractions; its paper subsequently describes ADT adapter-appending PCR and transcriptome tagmentation. Right large_cdna feeds existing prepare_transcriptome, prepare_tcr_ab and prepare_tcr_gd, with a custom two-step γ/δ enrichment alongside the 10× α/β workflow; small_tags feeds prepare_protein, prepare_hashtag and prepare_guide as separate PCRs. Existing sequence and associate_modalities then support transcript counts, antibody-binding reporter counts, sample identity, expressed-guide identity and receptor clonotype. Thus receptor variable-region readout plus direct guides and sample identity distinguish the ECCITE-seq architecture. These eight downstream right operations lie outside the selected historical core and have no left Operation counterpart. The core development assertion does not make the new branches inherited from Stoeckius CITE-seq (left b00014/b00036; right b00008/b00012/b00031–33). 7. Staining-panel adaptation within the existing label blocks: both papers use Fc blocking and cold incubation with barcoded antibodies. Right additionally reports empirically diluting oligo-labeled CD44/CD45 1:10 with untagged antibody because highly expressed markers can occupy excessive protein-library proportions; no equivalent dilution is reported for the left implementation. This changes the fraction of binding events carrying a DNA reporter for those markers and limits direct quantitative comparison of ADT counts between panels. This is an annotation to staining, not a new operation or an independently proven sensitivity improvement (left b00034; right b00026).

Reported consequence and limits

The earlier Drop-seq human/mouse CD29 experiment reported 97.2% agreement between transcriptome and ADT species classification of droplets (left b00015). ECCITE-seq demonstrated six readout branches in a PBMC/lymphoma-line/NIH-3T3 mixture, with guides detected in 93.5% of mouse cells; its separate K562 CRISPR experiment reported confident guides in 98.3% of cells after hashtag-based doublet removal. Combined hashing and guide detection distinguished 325 apparent two-guide assignments from 65 co-encapsulation doublets in the reported mixture (right b00012/b00014). In K562, the same-aliquot comparator was scRNA-seq alone, with no reduction in transcripts per cell; this is not a performance comparison to original CITE-seq and does not isolate the cause of guide recovery. The 49-marker healthy/CTCL analysis recovered receptor clonotypes and described expected marker enrichment as consistent with previous 3′ CITE-seq results, with that enrichment comparison 'not shown' (b00018). This supports demonstrated multimodal use and qualitative consistency, not equal sensitivity, superiority, safe reagent substitution, or causal benefit of click chemistry, TSO capture, or dilution. Authors' expectations about lower cost, super-loading and more robust future signatures are not measured gains over CITE-seq here.

Reviewer reasoning

The method_development definition is met: the later source documents a starting assay and a retained/revised connected arrangement. Mimitou Results b00011 explicitly says that CITE-seq method 7 was modified to combine protein markers with V(D)J and transcriptomes. Its reference 7 identifies Stoeckius, PMID 28759029, DOI 10.1038/nmeth.4380. I checked that earlier paper independently, including Results b00014, antibody construction b00025, copying/UMIs b00029, staining b00034, Drop-seq implementation b00036 and Figure 1b. This is procedural assay development, beyond an introductory citation or structural similarity. The supplied earlier graph is the Drop-seq implementation of the cited protein/transcript strategy; the earlier paper also reports 10× 3′ implementations in b00038. The claim explicitly avoids identifying Drop-seq hardware as a unique parent of the 10× 5P implementation. It does not publish component_adoption as a second label for this same assertion. The earlier connected arrangement starts with intact cells and antibody-identifying DNA conjugates. Antibody binding retains marker identity on cells that enter droplets with cell-barcoded oligo(dT) beads. Following lysis, both mRNA poly(A) and synthetic DNA poly(A) hybridize to bead poly(T). RNA-dependent synthesis copies mRNA, while the reverse transcriptase's DNA-dependent polymerase activity copies the antibody DNA. Bead-primer cell identifiers and UMIs associate the resulting DNA reporters with their compartment of origin. Initial amplification precedes Ampure separation into long cellular cDNA and short ADTs. These are DNA fractions for subsequent independent library generation, rather than direct protein sequences or completed measurements. Those connections are stated in the earlier paper, rather than reconstructed solely from the later account. The right retains surface-antibody labeling before droplet processing, cell-associated transcript/antibody recording, initial amplification and long/short fractionation, while making the coordinated revisions described in the claim. Its antibody inputs are irreversible covalent iEDDA conjugates with TSO-compatible DNA, replacing the earlier streptavidin–biotin/disulfide and poly(A)-tail design. Soluble poly(dT) primes mRNA RT; the bead-derived TSO supplies cell barcode/UMI through RNA template switching, while antibody DNA anneals and extends directly at the TSO interface. Right b00011 places barcode transfer within droplets, before recovery. Hashtag conjugates retain sample origin through intact-cell pooling, with amplification handles distinct from protein tags. When guide capture is desired, a scaffold-complementary RT primer copies the actual sgRNA variable sequence and obtains the compartment identifiers through template switching. Protein-, hashtag- and optional guide-tag additive primers support the amplified mixture. Right b00029–32 then connects this mixture to 0.6X SPRI: large cellular cDNA stays on beads and all three short reporter classes remain together in supernatant. Thus the retained strategy and changed material/information states are established at the claimed labeling-through-fractionation scope. Conjugate manufacture, timed cleavage and internal left sub-operations are not invented. The group tool confirms complete inputs and outputs for all five left Operations and nine right Operations. Left external inputs are cells, antibody_dna and oligobeads; its two output fractions are terminal. Right external inputs include all three cell streams, their respective hashtags, protein conjugates, TSO beads and soluble RT primers. Every output branch was inspected: large_cdna supplies prepare_transcriptome, prepare_tcr_ab and prepare_tcr_gd; small_tags supplies prepare_protein, prepare_hashtag and prepare_guide. Right b00031–32 independently supports these connections and the custom two-step γ/δ enrichment. The claim describes their downstream meaning without including the eight downstream right Operations in the historical core or attributing their invention to CITE-seq. Guide cDNA reports expressed-guide identity, antibody DNA reports binding, hashtags report sample origin and receptor-enriched cDNA supports clonotypes; guide presence alone does not establish a genomic edit. The staining adaptation is also represented as written: both papers describe Fc blocking and cold antibody incubation, while right b00026 reports diluting oligo-labeled CD44/CD45 with untagged antibody because some markers occupy excessive protein-library proportions. The record identifies this procedure and the authors' explanation without claiming a separately tested sensitivity benefit. Left 97.2% ADT/transcript species agreement, right 93.5% mouse-cell guide detection and right 98.3% K562 guide detection measure different quantities in different experiments. The 325/65 hashing/guide distinction is a reported joint assay result. The K562 same-aliquot comparator is scRNA-seq alone, and the healthy/CTCL statement calls marker enrichment consistent with previous 3′ CITE-seq while marking it not shown. These do not establish matched CITE-seq/ECCITE-seq performance or identify a causal gain from any single replacement. The claim preserves those limits as written.

    "left_evidence": [

    "claim_id": "assay_development",
    "decision": "accept",
    "rationale": "The method_development definition is met: the later source documents a starting assay and a retained/revised connected arrangement. Mimitou Results b00011 explicitly says that CITE-seq method 7 was modified to combine protein markers with V(D)J and transcriptomes. Its reference 7 identifies Stoeckius, PMID 28759029, DOI 10.1038/nmeth.4380. I checked that earlier paper independently, including Results b00014, antibody construction b00025, copying/UMIs b00029, staining b00034, Drop-seq implementation b00036 and Figure 1b. This is procedural assay development, beyond an introductory citation or structural similarity. The supplied earlier graph is the Drop-seq implementation of the cited protein/transcript strategy; the earlier paper also reports 10× 3′ implementations in b00038. The claim explicitly avoids identifying Drop-seq hardware as a unique parent of the 10× 5P implementation. It does not publish component_adoption as a second label for this same assertion.\n\nThe earlier connected arrangement starts with intact cells and antibody-identifying DNA conjugates. Antibody binding retains marker identity on cells that enter droplets with cell-barcoded oligo(dT) beads. Following lysis, both mRNA poly(A) and synthetic DNA poly(A) hybridize to bead poly(T). RNA-dependent synthesis copies mRNA, while the reverse transcriptase's DNA-dependent polymerase activity copies the antibody DNA. Bead-primer cell identifiers and UMIs associate the resulting DNA reporters with their compartment of origin. Initial amplification precedes Ampure separation into long cellular cDNA and short ADTs. These are DNA fractions for subsequent independent library generation, rather than direct protein sequences or completed measurements. Those connections are stated in the earlier paper, rather than reconstructed solely from the later account.\n\nThe right retains surface-antibody labeling before droplet processing, cell-associated transcript/antibody recording, initial amplification and long/short fractionation, while making the coordinated revisions described in the claim. Its antibody inputs are irreversible covalent iEDDA conjugates with TSO-compatible DNA, replacing the earlier streptavidin–biotin/disulfide and poly(A)-tail design. Soluble poly(dT) primes mRNA RT; the bead-derived TSO supplies cell barcode/UMI through RNA template switching, while antibody DNA anneals and extends directly at the TSO interface. Right b00011 places barcode transfer within droplets, before recovery. Hashtag conjugates retain sample origin through intact-cell pooling, with amplification handles distinct from protein tags. When guide capture is desired, a scaffold-complementary RT primer copies the actual sgRNA variable sequence and obtains the compartment identifiers through template switching. Protein-, hashtag- and optional guide-tag additive primers support the amplified mixture. Right b00029–32 then connects this mixture to 0.6X SPRI: large cellular cDNA stays on beads and all three short reporter classes remain together in supernatant. Thus the retained strategy and changed material/information states are established at the claimed labeling-through-fractionation scope. Conjugate manufacture, timed cleavage and internal left sub-operations are not invented.\n\nThe group tool confirms complete inputs and outputs for all five left Operations and nine right Operations. Left external inputs are cells, antibody_dna and oligobeads; its two output fractions are terminal. Right external inputs include all three cell streams, their respective hashtags, protein conjugates, TSO beads and soluble RT primers. Every output branch was inspected: large_cdna supplies prepare_transcriptome, prepare_tcr_ab and prepare_tcr_gd; small_tags supplies prepare_protein, prepare_hashtag and prepare_guide. Right b00031–32 independently supports these connections and the custom two-step γ/δ enrichment. The claim describes their downstream meaning without including the eight downstream right Operations in the historical core or attributing their invention to CITE-seq. Guide cDNA reports expressed-guide identity, antibody DNA reports binding, hashtags report sample origin and receptor-enriched cDNA supports clonotypes; guide presence alone does not establish a genomic edit.\n\nThe staining adaptation is also represented as written: both papers describe Fc blocking and cold antibody incubation, while right b00026 reports diluting oligo-labeled CD44/CD45 with untagged antibody because some markers occupy excessive protein-library proportions. The record identifies this procedure and the authors' explanation without claiming a separately tested sensitivity benefit. Left 97.2% ADT/transcript species agreement, right 93.5% mouse-cell guide detection and right 98.3% K562 guide detection measure different quantities in different experiments. The 325/65 hashing/guide distinction is a reported joint assay result. The K562 same-aliquot comparator is scRNA-seq alone, and the healthy/CTCL statement calls marker enrichment consistent with previous 3′ CITE-seq while marking it not shown. These do not establish matched CITE-seq/ECCITE-seq performance or identify a causal gain from any single replacement. The claim preserves those limits as written.",

scATAC-seq (combinatorial indexing) → sci-RNA-seq · component_adoption

Retained / shared arrangement

Scoped historical adoption of a collision-control procedure: choose limiting second-round compartment loading to control how often two or more compartments receive the same first/second well-barcode combination. The adopted contribution is this loading/design rule applied at redistribution, not the whole Cusanovich assay, its Tn5 chemistry, or the complete Cao library-preparation block.

What changed

Retained arrangement: first-indexed compartments are pooled and a limited number are redistributed to second-index wells; occupancy helps determine barcode-combination collisions. 1. Earlier implementation and destination: Cusanovich's combinatorial scATAC implementation redistributes intact nuclei carrying first-round Tn5-indexed genomic DNA; Cao applies the cited collision-control rule at split_second to fixed cells or recovered nuclei carrying RT-indexed cDNA. The function remains controlling ambiguous cellular assignment, while the molecular payload and next operation change from nuclear lysis/indexed PCR to second-strand cDNA synthesis followed by Tn5/lysis/indexed PCR (Cusanovich b00005–07; Cao b00009–10). 2. Reported implementation parameters: Cusanovich describes 15–25 nuclei per well in a second 96-well plate. Cao describes limiting FACS loading, e.g. 10–100 per well in 96- or 384-well plates, with DAPI singlet gating. These are the supported adaptations in context; the citation does not transfer the exact earlier sorting recipe or validate either range for a different sample.

Reported consequence and limits

The cited procedure addresses barcode collisions; it is not evidence that sci-RNA-seq has fewer collisions than scATAC. Cusanovich reports an estimated ~11% collision rate for the described experiments and separately validates species segregation. Cao's optimized 96 × 96 mixed-cell wells had 5 collisions among 294 transcriptomes, and mixed-nucleus wells had 3 among 175; these are different tested contexts and definitions, not a controlled earlier-versus-later gain. No independent benefit is assigned to occupancy, fixation, gate choice or indexing chemistry in isolation.

Reviewer reasoning

Accept component_adoption as written: this meets the shared definition of a named adopted contribution with an evidenced destination, not the definition of a documented starting assay revised into a later assay. The contribution is the second-well compartment-loading rule for controlling collisions of combinatorial cellular barcode identities. In Cao b00010 the sentence specifying tuning collisions by the number distributed to second wells directly cites reference 25; the parsed reference and b00072 identify it as Cusanovich's scATAC paper, PMID 25953818, DOI 10.1126/science.aab1601. Cao b00009 also describes actual limiting-number FACS loading, establishing use rather than a background citation alone. Independently, the earlier Cusanovich b00005 describes this same tunable collision property, b00007 implements 15–25 nuclei per second well after first-index tagging and pooling, and reports an estimated collision rate plus experimental species-segregation validation. At this claim's scope, the earlier arrangement begins with pooled intact nuclei retaining a Tn5-adapter first index; limiting redistribution creates second-well populations, followed by lysis and PCR adding the second index, allowing pooled genomic reads to be assigned by index pairs subject to collisions. The later destination is the existing split_second operation: pooled fixed cells or recovered nuclei retain RT-indexed first-strand cDNA, are loaded in limiting numbers into new wells, then undergo second-strand synthesis, Tn5/lysis and second-index PCR to yield pooled cDNA amplicons measured as expression tags. The retained occupancy rule controls ambiguity of the resulting two-well identities despite the changed payload and downstream processing. Cusanovich b00005–b00007 and Cao b00009–b00010 support those necessary connections; the group tool confirms the left three-way output and the right aggregate second-well boundary without missing side branches. The reported loading ranges, DAPI context and cell/nucleus choices describe the implementations and do not transfer an exact recipe or numerical collision curve. The left estimated ~11% and right mixed-species observed collisions (5/294 cells and 3/175 nuclei in the specified experiment) are accurate, explicitly different quantities and conditions, with no claimed comparative gain. All four reference-25 contexts were checked: introductory family attribution, the loading rule, the separate three-level indexed-Tn5 variant, and the discussion. None establishes the entire earlier assay as a documented starting implementation revised into this depicted two-level RNA route. Direction follows the procedural attribution and identified earlier demonstration; no duplicate historical label, dates-only ancestry or priority claim is used.

CITE-seq → MULTI-seq · component_adoption

Retained / shared arrangement

Adoption of the CITE-seq synthetic reporter design combining a PCR-amplification handle, an identifying barcode and a 3’ poly(A) domain compatible with oligo(dT) RNA capture. The earlier paper independently demonstrates antibody-bound DNA with these functions; the later Methods explicitly says the three-component sample barcode is designed 'as in Stoeckius et al51'. The component is present in the input antibody_dna to left bind_antibodies and in right index_reagents_a/b/c used by the labeling branches. The selected connected right group includes pool_cells to retain the complete three-branch boundary, but inheritance is restricted to reporter design, not scaffold assembly, cell labeling or pooling.

What changed

Retained arrangement: an amplifiable, identifying DNA reporter bears a poly(A) capture interface so that a non-RNA information channel can enter an oligo(dT)-based single-cell workflow. 1. Identity encoded: left the DNA identifies the antibody and thus its targeted epitope; right an 8 bp barcode, chosen with Hamming distance >3 among the utilized barcodes, identifies the sample assigned before pooling. Barcode abundance therefore has different interpretation on the two sides, even though both templates are synthetic DNA (left b00014; right b00045). 2. Placement of the reporter: left its 5’ end is attached to the antibody using the biotin/streptavidin and disulfide-bearing design; right the 5’ PCR handle also hybridizes to the anchor's complementary domain, and the reporter is held at the membrane by the separate anchor/co-anchor scaffold. Right retains the PCR-handle/barcode/poly(A) domain functions with a 30 bp poly(A) tail; this is not evidence that the entire left conjugate or every oligo sequence was transferred (left b00014/b00029; right b00012/b00045). 3. Modularity and use: left reporter identities distinguish members of an antibody panel applied to cells; right different sample-barcode oligos can be used with a single pair of anchor/co-anchor LMOs across separately labeled cultures that meet at pool_cells. The right modular scaffold is a distinct contribution with a separate citation, not a CITE-seq-derived antibody preparation procedure (right b00035/b00045).

Reported consequence and limits

The retained poly(A) domain supplies the interface accepted by subsequent bead capture; the retained PCR handle enables reporter amplification. These are demonstrated functions, not a claim of equivalent recovery for all DNA designs. Left demonstrated 97.2% agreement of transcript- and ADT-based species classifications in its intentionally multiplet-rich human/mouse Drop-seq experiment. Right demonstrated sample demultiplexing with the adapted reporter in the HEK/HMEC experiment. Neither result measures the causal gain from borrowing the three-domain design, nor compares protein quantification against sample assignment. Right modularity is an author-described design property; no quantitative advantage over the earlier antibody-conjugate design is isolated here.

Reviewer reasoning

Accepted as written under the component_adoption definition: a named adopted contribution, its earlier demonstration, and its destination in the later workflow are all established. The contribution is the synthetic reporter architecture containing an amplification handle, an identifying barcode and a 3’ poly(A) capture domain. Right b00045 explicitly describes these three components 'as in Stoeckius et al51'; right reference 51, checked in both the paper and parsed bibliography, identifies the supplied CITE-seq work, PMID 28759029 and DOI 10.1038/nmeth.4380. This is specific experimental design attribution, not an introductory citation or inference from dates. Left b00014 independently gives the three functions and demonstrates their use, b00029–b00032 document application-dependent handles and reporter designs, and b00015 reports the RNA/ADT species-classification experiment. The historical direction is therefore left to right at reporter-design scope. The scoped reconstruction is supported without adding graph operations. In the earlier arrangement, identifying polyadenylated DNA with a PCR handle is attached to an antibody, applied to cells and captured with mRNA through oligo(dT), then copied with cell attribution for separate reporter amplification. The component is present in the existing antibody_dna input to illumina:cite_seq:operation:bind_antibodies, rather than in an invented reporter-manufacture node. The later implementation retains those capture, identification and amplification functions, changes the encoded identity from antibody to assigned sample, specifies an 8 bp barcode and 30 bp poly(A) tail, and uses the 5’ PCR handle additionally as the hybridization interface to an anchor. The anchor/co-anchor carrier, not the antibody attachment, holds that reporter at the membrane. Right b00012 and b00045 establish these interfaces, and b00053 describes actual cell labeling and pooling. The group tool confirms that illumina:multi_seq:operation:label_a, illumina:multi_seq:operation:label_b, illumina:multi_seq:operation:label_c and illumina:multi_seq:operation:pool_cells take three cultures and their three reporter/scaffold preparations to one intact-cell pool. The reporter design is used by each labeling branch and carried through the pool to prepare_libraries. In that next block, right b00012 establishes poly(A)-mediated capture alongside transcripts and common cell-barcode linkage during copying, making sample identity available for demultiplexing. Thus the changed carrier and meaning still deliver the retained capture/amplification interfaces to their destination. Pooling, lipid-scaffold construction and antigen binding are expressly excluded from the inherited contribution. The claimed outcomes also remain at their demonstrated scopes: left 97.2% agreement is species classification in a deliberately multiplet-rich Drop-seq experiment; right validation is sample demultiplexing. Neither establishes equal quantitative readouts or a causal gain from adopting the design. The second historical claim concerns a distinct processing procedure, not another label for this reagent architecture. No complete-assay method_development assertion is made.

CITE-seq → MULTI-seq · component_adoption

Retained / shared arrangement

Adoption/adaptation of the CITE-seq reporter-plus-transcript library-processing contribution: generate and initially amplify cell-barcoded reporter DNA with transcript cDNA, separate the shorter reporter products from longer transcript-derived products, and prepare the channels independently. The right Methods explicitly bases its custom library protocol on 10X Genomics Single Cell V2 and CITE-seq reference 51. Earlier CITE-seq independently demonstrates the co-amplification/fractionation and separate finishing procedure in both its Drop-seq and 10× implementations. On the left this component is located in make_barcoded_dna plus size_separate, with library finishing documented only in the paper; on the right it is a component within prepare_libraries, not inheritance of that entire block or the whole assay.

What changed

Retained arrangement: shared cell attribution of transcript and reporter products, initial amplification followed by size separation, then independently completed libraries with adjustable sequencing allocation. 1. Named implementation: the left diagram depicts CITE-seq/Drop-seq; the cited CITE-seq paper additionally reports 10× Single Cell 3’ v1 and v2 workflows with the same reporter/cDNA fractionation strategy. Right uses a custom 10X Single Cell V2/CITE-seq-based protocol. Its attribution therefore supports the library contribution demonstrated by the earlier work, not descent of its hardware from the left Drop-seq arrangement (left b00036/b00038; right b00060). 2. Reporter amplification: the earlier left procedure describes initial cDNA amplification followed by size separation and further reporter-specific amplification; it does not describe the right additive-primer intervention during initial amplification. Right adds a MULTI-seq primer at cDNA amplification to recover barcode products that primed copying on capture beads but did not extend through template switching. This is a specific change inside right prepare_libraries versus the described earlier make_barcoded_dna procedure, not evidence that all reporter molecules follow the same template-switch path (left b00036/b00038; right b00060–b00062). 3. Fraction handling and next interface: left separates <300 nt ADTs from >300 nt transcript cDNA and amplifies the ADTs with primers appending Illumina P5/P7; the left drawing stops at those separate material fractions. Right uses 0.6X SPRI fractionation, applies small-RNA enrichment to remove contaminating oligonucleotides from its barcode fraction, performs barcode library PCR with TruSeq primers and further cleanup, and completes the expression channel through the 10X workflow. Right output states are finished libraries, not the left fraction states. No equivalence of the physical size cutoff or finishing primers is inferred, and the coordinated cleanup/primer workflow is not decomposed into independently beneficial changes (left b00036/b00038; right b00062–b00069). 4. Information represented: left the short channel counts antibody reporters as targeted protein proxies; right it counts sample indices for joining sample assignments to expression through a common cell barcode. Adopting library handling retains the information linkage, not the left protein measurement or its validation.

Reported consequence and limits

Both procedures permit separate treatment and sequencing allocation of their two channels. Right b00062 states that the additive primer increases barcode sequencing yield by enabling amplification of products lacking template-switch extension; this is the authors' reported consequence of that primer, with no numerical gain or direct CITE-seq comparator in the available passage. The same passage says the primer was accidentally omitted in the proof-of-concept snRNA-seq experiment and nuclei could still be robustly classified: the asserted yield effect is not a demonstrated requirement for successful classification. The present registered text describes Fig. S10C but does not supply a separately registered quantitative supplement for an independent effect-size assessment. No gain is assigned separately to small-RNA cleanup or to each coordinated library change. The papers' successful protein and sample readouts are validations of their respective combined assays, not proof that the adapted library workflow outperforms its donor.

Reviewer reasoning

Accepted as written under the component_adoption definition: the named adopted contribution is the reporter-plus-transcript library-processing procedure, and its destination is the custom library workflow within illumina:multi_seq:operation:prepare_libraries. Right b00060 expressly bases sequencing-library preparation on 10X Genomics Single Cell V2 and CITE-seq reference 51; the paper and parsed bibliography identify reference 51 as the supplied original CITE-seq work. Left b00014, b00029, b00036 and b00038 independently demonstrate reporter copying, initial amplification with transcript cDNA, size fractionation and separate library completion. The later attribution and earlier demonstration support this procedure's left-to-right adoption. They do not identify the complete antibody assay as the starting assay revised into MULTI-seq, so component_adoption, rather than complete-assay method_development, matches the assertion's scope. This processing contribution is distinct from the input reporter architecture in reporter_design_adoption; the two historical claims do not apply two labels to the same assertion. The before-to-after reconstruction is supported at that scope. Earlier CITE-seq carries oligo(dT)-captured mRNA and synthetic ADT DNA into copying with a common cell barcode; b00029 explains DNA-dependent copying of the DNA reporter. The Drop-seq procedure in b00036 initially amplifies cDNA for ten cycles, separates shorter ADTs from longer transcript cDNA with Ampure beads, further amplifies the short channel with primers adding P5/P7, and finishes the transcript channel by tagmentation. The 10× implementation in b00038 independently documents the same initial amplification/fractionation arrangement and v1/v2-dependent transcript-library finishing. Thus the procedure is genuinely demonstrated by the cited earlier work, including its 10× contexts; it is not reconstructed solely from the later paper. Within the unchanged left group, illumina:cite_seq:operation:make_barcoded_dna takes captured_lysate to mixed_dna and illumina:cite_seq:operation:size_separate outputs both mrna_dna_fraction and adt_dna_fraction. Independent finishing and subsequent sequencing are paper context beyond those boundary states. The later implementation retains common cell attribution, initial amplification, size separation and independent channel finishing while carrying sample-index DNA in the reporter channel. Necessary adaptations are recorded concretely in the submitted difference: the MULTI-seq additive primer is introduced during cDNA amplification to amplify barcodes that primed on capture beads but were not extended through template switching; a 0.6X SPRI split separates barcode-derived material and endogenous cDNA; the short fraction undergoes small-RNA enrichment to remove contaminating oligos, TruSeq-based library PCR and further cleanup; endogenous cDNA continues through the 10X workflow. Right b00060–b00069 establishes the order and next interfaces. These coordinated changes produce separate finished expression and sample-index libraries. The group tool confirms the pooled intact-cell input and both finished outputs, with both sequencing branches retained. These outputs serve expression measurement and sample classification via shared cell barcodes, rather than antibody-based protein measurement. No equivalence of size thresholds, finishing primers or template-switch routes is inferred. This accounts for the contribution and its destination without requiring a full recipe or inventing right internal IDs. The consequences are also appropriately qualified. Right b00062 attributes increased barcode sequencing yield to the additive-primer recovery mechanism; the claim identifies this as an author-reported effect with no numerical effect size or direct CITE-seq comparison. The same passage documents successful nuclei classification despite accidental omission of the primer, so it is not asserted to be necessary for classification. Separate quantitative supplementary evidence is unavailable in the registered packet. No independent benefit is assigned to each cleanup change, and successful assay readouts do not establish that the adapted procedure outperforms its donor. The comparison records the relevant procedure changes rather than replacing them with a generic recovery claim.

scM&T-seq → scNMT-seq · method_development

Retained / shared arrangement

Retained arrangement: Angermueller scM&T-seq physically separates mRNA and genomic DNA from the same individually processed cell before DNA bisulfite conversion, preparing an RNA-derived cDNA library separately from the scBS-seq DNA library. Clark scNMT-seq expressly builds on that arrangement and performs scM&T-seq library preparation after accessibility labelling. The selected connected groups cover cell disruption, separation, both library preparations and the DNA measurement route; their RNA-library outputs continue to the separately inspected RNA sequencing branch. The historical assertion concerns this parallel assay arrangement, not identity of every reagent or analysis component.

What changed

1. Initial treatment and its ordering (left lyse; right lyse_label): the Angermueller implementation collects cells directly into RLT plus lysis buffer with SUPERase-In. Clark instead collects cells into a methyltransferase reaction containing M.CviPI, S-adenosylmethionine, RNase inhibitor and 0.1% IGEPAL, incubates for 15 min at 37 °C, then adds RLT plus to stop the reaction and preserve RNA before scM&T-seq preparation. These coordinated conditions establish the labelling opportunity before RNA/DNA separation; no separate causal gain is assigned to enzyme, detergent, temperature or inhibitor. The right labelled_lysate and labelled_dna carry introduced GpC methylation in addition to endogenous methylation; the left cell_lysate and dna_fraction have no experimentally introduced accessibility record. Thus the same physical DNA branch acquires a second information-bearing feature, while the RNA branch remains physically separated before bisulfite conversion. 2. DNA-library implementation within the retained branch (left bisulfite_prep; right dna_prep): left Methods specify EZ Methylation Direct reagent on purified DNA in the presence of AMPure XP beads, magnetic-bead purification/desulphonation on a Bravo, 3′-random-hexamer primers for first and second strand synthesis, omission of first-strand biotin capture, and an extra purification before PCR. Right Methods specify EZ Methylation Direct MagBead conversion at half volumes followed by iterative 6NF-primed Klenow synthesis, exonuclease I treatment, second-strand synthesis and indexed PCR. Both convert DNA before library synthesis. These are source descriptions inside existing broad blocks, not newly extracted suboperations. The later paper calls its preparation a version of published G&T-seq and scBS-seq protocols with minor modifications; it does not identify every listed difference as an innovation relative to the 2016 implementation. In particular, the later use of 6NF does not establish a new primer design, and the left paper's shorter account does not establish absence of unmentioned synthesis steps. 3. DNA read interpretation (left align_dna; right separate_dna_signals, downstream of sequence_dna/dna_sequence): left Bismark processing yields methylation calls and CpG methylation summaries. Right processing adds the Bismark coverage2cytosine --NOMe option, retaining A–C–G/T–C–G for endogenous CpG methylation and G–C–A/G–C–C/G–C–T for GpC accessibility. G–C–G is ambiguous between endogenous and introduced methylation, and C–C–G is excluded for possible off-target enzyme activity. The right two DNA endpoints are interpretations of the same bisulfite dataset, not separately isolated chromatin and methylation fractions. This coordinated marking-plus-context interpretation is what adds accessibility to the retained two-library arrangement.

Reported consequence and limits

The combined scNMT-seq workflow supported joint transcription, endogenous methylation and accessibility profiling: 61 of 70 treated serum-grown EL16 ESCs passed both RNA-seq and BS-seq QC in the initial validation. The left original study reported 75 of 92 cells passing both filters, including 61 serum E14 ESCs and 14 2i ESCs. These are different biological samples, sequencing schemes and QC rules, so the fractions do not demonstrate an efficiency gain. The right context exclusions reduce theoretically assayable CpG cytosines from approximately 22 million to 11 million. The right authors explain that GpC encoding permits covered unmarked sites to be distinguished from missing coverage; that explanation does not establish equal sensitivity or safe substitution for scM&T-seq. No experiment in the registered text assigns a separate performance benefit to each preparative difference in entry 2.

Reviewer reasoning

Accepted as written under method_development: a documented starting assay with a retained and revised arrangement. Clark b00006 names scM&T-seq and describes the separation-before-bisulfite/Smartseq2 arrangement; its reference 3 (b00081) identifies Angermueller, DOI 10.1038/nmeth.3728, PMID 26752769. This is actual attribution, reinforced by Clark b00032's 'prior to scM&T-seq library preparation', rather than a conclusion from dates. Independently, Angermueller b00005 and b00015-17 establish the earlier arrangement: a cell collected directly into RLT plus/SUPERase-In is processed into physically separated mRNA and genomic DNA; DNA is eluted, bisulfite-converted before strand synthesis/PCR, and prepared separately from the RNA-derived cDNA/Nextera XT library. In Clark, the cell first enters the IGEPAL-containing M.CviPI/SAM reaction, then RLT plus stops the reaction and preserves RNA. Capture still routes bead-associated RNA to cDNA/library preparation and genomic DNA to conversion and scBS-seq synthesis. The latter material now carries endogenous methylation plus introduced GpC marking. One DNA library and its reads feed the context-restricted Bismark reports, yielding endogenous CpG methylation and GpC accessibility; the separate RNA library continues to RNA sequencing/expression measurement. These necessary connections are supported by Clark b00009, b00032, b00038 and Angermueller b00005, b00015-22. Both selected groups are connected. Their boundary inputs include the isolated cell and capture support; the RNA-library side output and both right DNA-data outputs are preserved. The claim covers the necessary coordinated treatment/order and interpretation changes, and correctly distinguishes the documented DNA-preparation descriptions from unproved innovations or absences on the earlier side. No internal operation IDs are invented. The changed sample contexts and QC rules prevent treating 75/92 versus 61/70 as a tested efficiency gain. Kelly/NOMe attribution is separately identified as context, without assigning its marking contribution to scM&T-seq or duplicating this assertion as component_adoption. No necessary change at this claimed scope is omitted.

iCLIP (Huppertz protocol) → irCLIP · method_development

Retained / shared arrangement

Scoped development from the UV-C iCLIP experimental strategy described in Huppertz (PMID 24184352; right reference 16) into Zarnegar irCLIP (PMID 27111506). The retained arrangement is UV capture of in-cell RBP–RNA contacts, antibody enrichment, RNA 3′ adapter ligation, gel/nitrocellulose RNP purification, proteinase-K recovery of peptide-bearing RNA, and an RT-stop-compatible, barcoded cDNA circularization/PCR library strategy. The affected portion is the experimental route from cellular RNP preparation through RNA recovery and library construction. The whole existing operation groups locate that scope; they do not assert that every operation, reagent or branch was inherited unchanged.

What changed

Retained arrangement: selected UV-crosslinked RNP → 3′-adapter-bearing peptide–RNA → RT-stop-encoding cDNA → circular-template PCR library. Distinct changes, using only existing records: 1. Preparation context (left crosslink/lyse_fragment; right o_crosslink): Huppertz specifies 150 mJ/cm² UV-C, standard lysis and optional sonication, whereas right specifies 0.3 J/cm² UV-C and 1% SDS lysis/sonication before dilution and IP (left b00039, b00051–53; right b00019). These differences are documented context, not separately proven innovations or gains. 2. Digestion placement and choice (left lyse_fragment/immunoprecipitate; right o_ip/o_digest): left partial RNase I acts in lysate before IP; right trims captured complexes on beads, separately testing RNase A/RNase 1 and preferring RNase A for optimal fragment recovery. Right b00006 explicitly names iCLIP16 as the in-lysate baseline; the target-size contexts also differ (left 50–300-nt RNA versus right about 40–100-nt RNA). 3. Detection and affinity interface (left dephosphorylate/ligate_label; right o_ligate, with downstream o_rt): left ligates preadenylated L3 DNA and adds a 5′-RNA radiotracer to a subset; right ligates preadenylated DNA containing internal IR800CW and terminal 3′ biotin. The changed dye carrier reports 3′ ligation in RNP material and its biotin later captures RNA–cDNA hybrids (left b00064–70; right b00004, b00015, b00019, b00026). PNK end preparation and RNA 3′ ligation are retained at the stated scope. 4. Membrane RNA recovery (left separate_isolate/proteinase_k; right o_recover): the 37 °C PK/added-urea procedure is replaced by PK in 0.2% SDS at 50 °C. Right b00007 explicitly cites standard urea recovery15,16 and evaluates its reformulation. This combined condition change preserves peptide-bearing RNA but changes release/precipitation behavior (left b00075; right b00021). 5. cDNA conversion (left reverse_transcribe; right o_rt): SSIII with 42/50 °C extension becomes TGIRT-III with 60 °C extension; right b00008–09 assesses losses in iCLIP library construction and tests this enzyme replacement. RT-associated contact endpoints remain used rather than being eliminated. 6. Primer/template information and PCR design (left reverse_transcribe and all linearize_pcr_* branches; right o_rt/o_library): left uses a 5-random/4-sample-nt RT barcode and cleavable regions with Cut_oligo/BamHI circle linearization; right uses a 17-nt composite degenerate barcode with a 6-bp TruSeq sample barcode and two internal carbon spacers needed for linear PCR product from circular templates, with no stated BamHI step (left b00079, b00086; right b00023, b00026). This locates a molecular-design difference without assigning it an unverified donor or performance effect. 7. cDNA purification and selection arrangement (left remove_rna_select, all circularize_*/linearize_pcr_* and pool_libraries; right o_rt/o_library): left hydrolyzes RNA, cuts three cDNA gel bands, processes fractions separately and merges amplified fractions. Right affinity-captures template–cDNA hybrids, releases cDNA, circularizes and performs magnetic-bead cleanup plus size selection of library products within an 8-h two-tube design (left b00077, b00083–94; right b00009, b00026). The shared circularization strategy does not transfer the left three-fraction branch structure to the right.

Reported consequence and limits

The right main paper reports a coordinated package: faster infrared visualization, more optimal-sized RNA with on-bead trimming, improved RNA recovery with the joint PK/precipitation reformulation, four- to eight-fold greater cDNA output in its TGIRT-versus-SSIII assay, and two- to three-fold greater cleanup recovery versus its silica-column control. These are distinct tested contexts, not additive estimates of the package's benefit over the exact left graph. Its 8-h timing concerns conversion of precipitated RNA to dsDNA. The authors attribute lower cellular-input requirements to cumulative methodological improvements; their hnRNP-C sequencing comparison is with published iCLIP data, primarily reference 9, as qualified in c09. No gain is inferred from the directed historical relation itself or assigned to primer spacers, a specific branch deletion, or any one unisolated factor.

Reviewer reasoning

Accepted as method_development under the shared definition of a documented starting assay with a retained/revised arrangement. This is not inferred from dates or structural similarity. Zarnegar b00006 specifically attributes in-lysate digestion to iCLIP16; b00007 attributes the standard urea/37 °C membrane-PK recovery to references 15,16; b00008 actually assesses iCLIP library construction with irCLIP-adapter-bearing RNA and a bypass cDNA control; b00009 describes replacements after this loss assessment, and b00010 incorporates the changes into a coherent irCLIP protocol. The paper's reference 16 is Huppertz PMID 24184352. Together these procedural contexts establish the cited iCLIP experimental strategy as a development baseline, while also acknowledging HITS-CLIP and other contributions. I independently checked the earlier implementation: Huppertz b00008, b00039, b00049–86 and b00094 establish UV-C capture, in-lysate RNase I, antibody-bead selection, PNK/3′ L3 ligation plus a 5′ radiotracer subset, gel/nitrocellulose selection, PK/urea release of peptide–RNA, SSIII synthesis, alkaline RNA removal, three gel-separated cDNA fractions, circularization, BamHI linearization, PCR and pooling. This is a connected arrangement demonstrated by the earlier source, not reconstructed solely from the later description. At the affected cell-preparation-through-library scope, irCLIP retains UV contact capture and antibody attribution, PNK/3′ DNA-adapter ligation, gel/membrane selection, peptide-bearing RNA release, RT-stop information and cDNA circularization/PCR. It changes the lysis/UV context, relocates RNA trimming onto captured beads with separately tested nucleases, replaces the radioactive tracing interface with the IR800–biotin adapter, jointly changes PK formulation/temperature, uses TGIRT-III/60 °C, revises barcode/spacer design and replaces the three-fraction gel/merge route with hybrid affinity capture, cDNA release and bead-based library processing. Zarnegar b00015, b00019, b00021, b00023 and b00026 support the material connections: RNA-linked adapter survives RNP selection and PK release; its biotin retains RNA–cDNA hybrids on streptavidin; released, primer-bearing linear cDNA is circularized and amplified into dsDNA carrying insert/barcode/RT-endpoint information for subsequent sequencing. The changed signal carrier reports adapter ligation, rather than becoming the cDNA sequence carrier. All necessary coordinated changes are present in the submitted description; no separately inherited fraction arrangement is asserted. The whole-group boundaries include all left side inputs and branches and the independent right adapter input. Reported recovery, cDNA and timing results retain their tested comparators and are not combined into an exact-Huppertz performance estimate. There is no overlapping component_adoption label for this assertion, no sole-parent or priority claim, and no implied transfer to an untested hybrid workflow.

ATAC-seq → scATAC-seq (combinatorial indexing) · method_development

Retained / shared arrangement

Scoped assay development from Buenrostro et al. ATAC-seq (PMID 24097267; DOI 10.1038/nmeth.2688) to Cusanovich et al. combinatorial-indexing scATAC-seq (PMID 25953818). Retained arrangement: adapter-loaded Tn5 acts on nuclear chromatin, preferential insertion records accessible genomic locations as tagged DNA fragments, and PCR of those adapter-bearing genomic templates produces a DNA sequencing library. The affected portion runs from prepared nuclear chromatin through tagged DNA to amplified library; it excludes a claim of inherited nuclei-isolation procedure. The right connected group includes the new indexing routes as revisions of this arrangement, not as transformations individually shared with the left.

What changed

Retained arrangement: nuclear-chromatin tagmentation followed by PCR of the tagged genomic DNA, with genomic insert positions and lengths carrying accessibility information. 1. First-round tagging and index carrier: left tagmentation produces adapter-tagged DNA from a bulk nuclear preparation, with no two-round cellular-index scheme in this implementation. Right split_first and tagment_top/tagment_middle/tagment_bottom distribute nuclear populations among first-round wells and insert well-barcoded adapters with transposase. The first index is physically on tagged nuclear DNA and identifies a well shared by many nuclei, rather than a unique cell by itself. The three drawn routes are illustrative; the reported experiment uses 96 first-round wells. This changes what information accompanies each genomic fragment into the next pooling step. Support: left Results b00008–b00009; right b00005–b00007 and Fig. 1A. 2. Material retained and handling between tagging and PCR: the left standard purification_amplification block follows transposition with DNA purification and amplification. Right pool_nuclei and split_second instead pool and dilute intact tagged nuclei, then use a cell sorter to redistribute 15–25 nuclei into each of 96 second-round wells before nuclear lysis. Retaining nuclear compartments preserves DNA-to-nucleus association while redistributing first-round indexes; this is not pooling free DNA. The left 500- and 5,000-cell variants already bypass pre-PCR Minelute purification, so omission of that cleanup alone is not established as a new right-side invention. Support: left Methods b00026–b00029; right b00005 and b00007. 3. Second-round attribution and product pooling: left purification_amplification amplifies the tagged bulk library with custom Nextera-compatible primers; the reported qPCR monitoring stops amplification before saturation. Right pcr_barcode_top/pcr_barcode_middle/pcr_barcode_bottom lyse the redistributed nuclei and add a second well index during PCR with primers complementary to the first-stage adapters; pool_library then combines indexed DNA products. The combination of first-round DNA adapter index and second-round PCR index, retained in the DNA library, is used to group reads by putative cell. Neither well alone must contain a single nucleus, and repeated index pairs cause collisions. The right source does not establish retention of the left qPCR schedule or cleanup procedure. Support: left b00027; right b00005–b00007 and Fig. 1A. 4. Measurement unit and interpretation: left library molecules collectively report population accessibility, with paired-end insert-size and insertion-position analyses also supporting nucleosome and factor-occupancy inference. Right indexed molecules permit sparse per-nucleus DHS-use profiles; cell-type quantitative accessibility is estimated across many cells. Its attribution architecture, rather than simply use of Tn5 or an extra barcode, makes this distinction meaningful. Sequencing and analysis are paper annotations to the DNA endpoints, not newly extracted operations. Support: left b00011, b00016 and b00021; right b00003, b00012–b00013.

Reported consequence and limits

Observed for the combined right workflow: the paper reports maps for >15,000 single cells across experiments, not per assay. In the initial human GM12878/mouse Patski mixture, ~93% of 533 retained barcode combinations had >90% of reads assigned to one species. The ~11% collision rate is an estimate for the described experiments, not a guarantee of unique cell identity or the same statistic as species purity. Author explanation: intact-nucleus redistribution and two index rounds make most well combinations specific to one nucleus without individualized single-cell reactions. No isolated causal gain is established for any one change, and no improvement over bulk sensitivity or molecular recovery follows from this development relation. The direct bulk comparisons are recorded separately in C4.

Reviewer reasoning

Accept the submitted method_development claim as written. It meets the shared definition of a documented starting assay with a retained and revised connected arrangement. Cusanovich b00006 explicitly integrates combinatorial cellular indexing with ATAC-seq and procedurally describes that assay with references 4 and 20. Reference 4 is independently identified as Buenrostro, PMID 24097267, DOI 10.1038/nmeth.2688. The integration passage plus actual implementation in b00007 establishes the historical assertion; neither the introductory citation, assay name, comparative results nor publication dates is being used to infer descent. The separately attributed indexing contributions in references 17/18 are not assigned to Buenrostro, and the record does not duplicate this assertion with component_adoption. The earlier implementation was checked independently in Buenrostro b00008-b00009 and Online Methods b00025-b00029: prepared unfixed nuclei supply chromatin DNA; adapter-loaded Tn5 fragments and tags that DNA; the standard preparation purifies the transposition products and amplifies the library with Nextera primers, followed by cleanup. The 500/5,000-cell variants already take transposition products directly into PCR. The submitted record retains this qualification and does not misidentify omission of pre-PCR Minelute purification as an invention by the later work. The retained strategy is accessibility-biased nuclear-DNA tagmentation followed by adapter-directed PCR. Its genomic insertion locations and fragment lengths supply the subsequent sequencing readout, supported by Buenrostro b00011/b00021. The later revision is reconstructed at the claimed scope from Cusanovich b00005-b00007 and Fig. 1A: nuclei are distributed among first wells; barcoded transposase attaches the first well index to genomic DNA within nuclei; intact tagged nuclei are pooled, diluted and sorted at 15-25 nuclei per second well; only after redistribution are nuclei lysed and DNA amplified with adapter-complementary primers that introduce the second well index. PCR products, rather than intact nuclei, are then pooled. This yields a genomic DNA library bearing index combinations used to associate reads with putative cells. The nuclear carrier before lysis and the indexed DNA product after PCR are consequential, and both are preserved in the submitted difference. Many nuclei share a first index, second wells need not be single-nucleus wells, and collision limits qualify the cellular assignment. The main text directly supplies these connections; no detailed lysis chemistry or unreported cleanup schedule has been assumed. All coordinated changes necessary for this stated arrangement are covered, including the distinct two pooling events, lysis placement and changed measurement unit. The required group inspections confirm exact existing IDs and complete boundaries. The left two-operation group consumes native_chromatin plus tn5_transposome and yields dna_library, with tagged_fragments internal. The right ten-operation group consumes nuclei and yields pooled_dna_library; all three first-round and all three second-round branches are internal. Its only surrounding operation is isolate_nuclei, which is explicitly excluded from the claimed inheritance. No side route is omitted. The approximately 93% species-purity observation for 533 retained combinations and approximately 11% estimated collision rate describe different quantities; the submitted consequence correctly separates them and attributes validation to the combined workflow. More than 15,000 cells is the total across experiments, and no individual revision receives a causal performance gain. Sequencing and interpretation remain paper annotations to physical DNA endpoints. This is sufficiently expressed by both existing diagrams with the submitted paper annotations.
