ATAC-seq → THS-seq · method_development

Retained / shared arrangement

Scoped assay development from Buenrostro et al.'s original ATAC-seq implementation (PMID 24097267, DOI 10.1038/nmeth.2688) to Sos et al.'s THS-seq implementation (PMID 26846207). Retained arrangement: sample accessibility by in vitro Tn5 insertion into native cellular chromatin, retain insertion-associated genomic sequence through library preparation, and obtain amplified Illumina DNA libraries to map accessible chromatin. The affected portion is initial chromatin tagging through library completion: both existing ATAC-seq operations versus the connected five-operation THS-seq route. Retention of this assay strategy does not assert retention of each downstream reaction.

What changed

1. Tag design and usable ends, at left tagmentation versus right insert: left Tn5 transfers sequencing adapters and yields fragments amplified with two Nextera-compatible primers; right custom transposons contain mosaic ends, a T7 promoter and an Illumina-compatible read-primer/adaptor sequence. The right dimer/cargo design is intended to make each insertion yield usable ends in either orientation. Sos explains the left PCR design's losses by adapter orientation and spacing; Buenrostro independently demonstrates the earlier adapter-insertion/PCR arrangement, but does not independently quantify those losses (left b00008, b00026–27; right b00007, b00010). 2. Initial transposase, at those same insertion records: left uses hyperactive Illumina Tn5; right replaces what Sos calls standard EzTn5 with engineered Tn5059 for chromatin tagging. Higher activity and lower sequence bias are reported for purified DNA and cannot by themselves establish the chromatin benefit. Right's later cDNA tagmentation still uses EzTn5; this is not an all-workflow replacement by Tn5059 (left b00008, b00026; right b00010, b00036). 3. Initial reaction context, at left tagmentation versus right insert: left standard preparation uses isolated nuclei and Illumina TD reaction buffer; right adds custom transposomes to gently lysed cells in a defined buffer with 16 % DMF. Both papers use 37 °C for 30 minutes for initial transposition. The original TD formulation is unspecified, so the contrast is a documented defined/optimized right formulation, not proof that the original contained zero DMF (left b00025–26; right b00032). No separate causal gain is assigned to buffer alone. 4. Recovery interface, at left purification_amplification versus right purify and fill_ivt: the left standard preparation purifies transposed DNA with Minelute and uses it for genomic-fragment PCR; right removes proteins with guanidine hydrochloride, recovers tagged DNA with SPRI beads and retains DNA with the beads for gap filling and same-tube IVT. The original left 500/5,000-cell variant omits pre-PCR Minelute, which is a paper qualification of the fixed broad block, not a new operation (left b00026–29; right b00032–34). 5. Amplification unit and material route, at left purification_amplification versus right fill_ivt and synthesize_cdna: left directly PCR-amplifies the genomic intervals between compatible adapter ends; right first fills DNA gaps and makes RNA copies of insertion-site flanks from the introduced T7 promoter, then converts that RNA to double-stranded cDNA. The DNA template is used to produce RNA copies, not chemically converted into RNA. This changes the first amplification interface from a PCR-compatible pair of genomic ends to a promoter-bearing end and subsequent RNA-to-cDNA processing. Sos reports roughly 1,000-fold IVT and independence from neighboring insertion spacing, while describing lower bias than exponential PCR as theoretical (left b00008, b00027; right b00010, b00034–36). 6. Library completion and information carrier, at left purification_amplification versus right add_adaptors: left's amplified library contains original tagged genomic inserts; right fragments/tags IVT-derived double-stranded cDNA with custom EzTn5 transposomes, then uses protease, gap fill-in and PCR to add Illumina adaptors and a sample index. Right's second transposition tags cDNA 3′ ends and is not another chromatin-accessibility observation. A Tn5059 substitute at this second step is proposed but explicitly was not used (left b00027, b00038; right b00010, b00036). 7. Product selection/readout interpretation, annotated at the same library-output records: left avoids size selection and uses paired-end genomic inserts, whose lengths support nucleosome inference. Right's paper reports pooling sample-indexed libraries and gel selection of approximately 220–1,000-bp products, followed by single-end sequencing of insertion-site-derived cDNA. This library finishing/readout context is an annotation, not additional graph operations. Right sample barcodes identify samples; they are not demonstrated molecule or cell identifiers. RNA/cDNA here is a reporter of genomic insertion sites, not endogenous RNA abundance (left b00011, b00031, b00038; right b00012, b00036, b00038).

Reported consequence and limits

The relation establishes a documented revision of an assay strategy, not a gain by definition. Sos's experiments report gains for the combined THS-seq/Tn5059 workflow in limited-input GM12878 cells and nonadditive interactions of workflow and enzyme (C5). They do not isolate a gain for every numbered change. The claim that IVT has less bias than exponential PCR is stated as theory; explanations based on orientation, spacing, enzyme selectivity and fragment-length preferences remain author interpretations at the stated evidence level. Sample-level library indexing does not establish single-cell attribution, UMI-based molecule counting or preservation of original genomic fragment lengths.

Reviewer reasoning

The method_development definition is met: this is a documented starting assay with a retained and revised arrangement, not adoption of an isolated contribution. Sos identifies the ATAC-seq implementation through reference 5/CR5 (PMID 24097267; DOI 10.1038/nmeth.2688). Its actual citation contexts in b00006 and b00014–15 identify the established assay and its published 500-cell datasets; the introductory citation and comparisons alone would be insufficient history. The necessary development support is the connected argument in b00007: the authors identify immediate PCR recovery, buffer and transposase as three aspects of ATAC-seq to revise, explicitly undertake optimization of all three, and implement the resulting arrangement in b00010. Discussion b00025 explicitly describes changing ATAC-seq to THS-seq. This supports the stated direction independently of dates. Buenrostro b00008 and b00025–29 independently establish the earlier assay: loaded Tn5 inserts sequencing adapters into genomic chromatin, and the tagged genomic intervals feed purification and Nextera-primer PCR; the original low-cell route omits the pre-PCR cleanup. At the claimed scope, accessibility-dependent chromatin insertion and retention of insertion-associated genomic sequence in an amplified Illumina library are retained. The submitted seven differences account for the necessary coordinated revisions: isolated nuclei/TD-buffer/Illumina Tn5 become gently lysed-cell insertion with custom T7/read-primer cargo, initial Tn5059 and defined 16 % DMF conditions; standard Minelute-to-genomic-PCR recovery becomes guanidine treatment and SPRI recovery with DNA retained with beads for gap filling and same-tube IVT. The promoter-bearing DNA templates generate RNA copies of insertion flanks, rather than themselves becoming RNA; those copies are converted to double-stranded cDNA through first-strand synthesis, RNA removal and second-strand synthesis. Sos b00032–36 establishes these material and carrier connections. The cDNA then feeds the distinct, actually implemented EzTn5/ME_BOT/ME_TOP transposition, followed by protease, gap fill-in and barcode/adaptor PCR, yielding a sample-indexed DNA library. The proposed Tn5059 replacement at this second transposition was not used. The stated size-selection and paired-end genomic-interval versus single-end insertion-flank readouts correctly qualify the final outputs and avoid equating original fragment-length information. Thus the conceptual route to the next operation and measurement is evidenced on both sides without requiring an executable protocol or unrepresented predecessor IDs. The group tool confirms the exact two-operation left selection and five-operation right selection are complete connected routes: chromatin plus loaded transposome enter, DNA libraries leave, and no side branch is omitted. No hidden reaction receives an invented ID. The change-coverage audit found no necessary omitted revision within this scoped assertion. C2 correctly separates the measured combined-workflow outcomes from theoretical lower IVT bias and proposed orientation/spacing, enzyme-selectivity and length explanations. It neither attributes the T7 system or new enzyme to Buenrostro nor counts the retained assay strategy again as component_adoption.

ATAC-seq → ChIPmentation · component_adoption

Retained / shared arrangement

The inherited contribution is specifically the use of the custom Nextera PCR-primer procedure for amplification of tagmented sequencing libraries, demonstrated in Buenrostro et al.'s original ATAC-seq and explicitly attributed to ATAC-seq reference 4 in Schmidl et al.'s amplification subsection that includes ChIPmentation. The destination is the amplification component inside the right composite recovery/purification/amplification operation. No adoption of the whole block or ATAC-seq assay is asserted.

What changed

Retained arrangement at the component scope: a Tn5-tagged genomic fragment is amplified using the custom Nextera primers, whose adapter-compatible priming function is used to generate the sequencing library (left b00027; right b00025-b00026). 1. Context: left custom Nextera PCR primers 1 and 2 amplify purified products of native-chromatin accessibility profiling; right custom Nextera primers amplify DNA recovered after on-bead immunoprecipitated-chromatin tagmentation, elution and reverse-crosslinking. The primer procedure is retained in a changed sample-selection and recovery context; the later paper does not identify a new primer design. 2. Surrounding amplification: left uses NEBnext master mix and an initial-five-cycle/aliquot-qPCR arrangement; right uses KAPA HiFi HotStart, activation for nick translation, and qPCR-derived final enrichment cycles (C2; left b00027, right b00026). These documented context changes are not evidence that all PCR conditions, recovery chemistry, bead handling or size selection came from the earlier assay. The historical assertion is restricted to the cited custom-primer component within the exact selected existing operations.

Reported consequence and limits

Actual library preparation with the attributed primer procedure is reported in ChIPmentation, so adoption and use are established. Neither paper isolates the effect of those primers against an alternative primer design in this paired context. No inherited gain in sensitivity, complexity, bias, cell-input tolerance or speed is attributed to this component. ChIPmentation's broader workflow results cannot be assigned to primer adoption.

Reviewer reasoning

Accepted as written. The component_adoption definition is met: the named adopted contribution is use of the custom Nextera PCR primers for library amplification, and its destination is amplification inside illumina:chipmentation:operation:purification_amplification. Right b00026 explicitly attributes actual use: 'Library preparation was performed using custom Nextera primers as described for ATAC-seq4.' The heading b00025 includes ChIPmentation, and right reference 4 identifies Buenrostro et al., PMID 24097267, DOI 10.1038/nmeth.2688. Independently, left b00026-b00027 describes purified transposition products being amplified using custom Nextera PCR primers 1 and 2. Thus the direction is established by procedural attribution and the cited implementation, not dates or the shared Tn5 structure. At the adopted component's scope, the earlier arrangement starts with Tn5-tagged genomic fragments, purifies them, and uses the named primers to amplify a sequencing library. What is retained on the right is the named primer use with tagmented library DNA. The changed destination material originates in antibody-captured, bead-bound chromatin; right b00022 supplies the necessary connection through elution, reverse-crosslinking and DNA purification before amplification. Right b00026 then establishes final enrichment and sequencing. The resulting amplified genomic DNA serves sequencing of target-enriched regions rather than the earlier accessibility-selected population. The documented NEBnext versus KAPA and cycle-estimation differences are surrounding procedure changes; the later text reports no new primer design. This reconstruction requires no inferred identity of oligonucleotide sequences, no adoption of the whole composite operation, and no transfer of performance. It is not a duplicate historical label for C5: C3 publishes only this primer contribution, whereas C5 withholds whole-assay development.
